7.4.
Future Work

7.4.1.
Substructural Types and Effects

Currently, our treatment of effects is relatively primitive, in that, while we postulate a lattice
of effects, our equational theory only distinguishes between
pure
and
impure

functions, the latter having effect
\bot
.
Führmann (1999)
studies languages with
semantics given in an
abstract Kleisli category
. In particular, he introduces the notion of

•

Central
operations, which commute with all other operations; we like to call such
morphisms
linear

•

Duplicable
operations
f
, for which
\mathsf{let}\;y=f(x);\;(y,y)\approx(f(x),f(x))

•

Discardable
operations
f
, for which
\mathsf{let}\;y=f(x);\;e\approx e
when
e
does
not depend on
y

While, as we make use of in our calculus, pure operations are central, duplicable, and discardable,
it can be useful to study each notion in isolation, as well as to consider morphisms which are
central and duplicable (which we have taken to calling
relevant
) and morphisms which are
both central and discardable (which we have taken to calling
affine
). Examples of morphisms
which are not pure but nonetheless relevant or affine abound; for example,

•

In a programming language with nondeterminism, nondeterministic functions are
impure

(since
\mathsf{let}\;y=f(x);\;(y,y)
is a strict refinement of
(f(x),f(x))
), but are nontheless

affine
, because discarding the result of a nondeterministic function does not affect the
program’s behavior, assuming they have no other effect.

•

In a programming language with nontermination, nonterminating functions with no other effect
are impure (since
\mathsf{let}\;y=f(x);\;e
may diverge even if
e
does not) but are always
duplicable, because duplicating a nonterminating function does not introduce new effects beyond
divergence. Depending on whether the language’s other effects commute with nontermination, they
may in fact be relevant.

All three of these concepts still make perfect sense when interpreted as-is in a Freyd category, and
are particularly useful when reasoning about models of probabilistic programming, such as Markov
categories (which have many affine morphisms)
(
nLab authors, 2024
)
.

Given that such relevant and affine side-effects have substitution principles which depend on how
often a variable is used in a term, we call such side-effects
substructural
. We might also
consider how we could support substructural, and in particular linear,
types
. Categorically,
this corresponds to weakening the requirement that our base category be cartesian, instead requiring
only a symmetric monoidal category. The resulting generalization of a Freyd category is called an

effectful category
by
Román (2023)
.

7.4.2.
Refinement and Enrichment

Throughout this work, we have focused on the notion of program
equivalence

\Gamma\vdash r\approx r^{\prime}\rhd{\mathsf{L}}
. In many settings, especially when considering the nondeterminism and
concurrency, we would also like to study program
refinement

\Gamma\vdash r\sqsubseteq r^{\prime}\rhd\mathsf{L}
. Semantically, this corresponds to an enrichment of our model in the category of
partial orders; we conjecture that adding this to our semantics would require minimal changes. We
may also consider how our syntax could be extended to support explicit parallelism and how we could
reflect the corresponding algebraic laws (as given in, e.g.,
Hoare and van Staden (2014)
), such as

(111)

(P\parallel Q);(R\parallel S)\sqsubseteq(P;R)\parallel(Q;S)

in our equational theory. More generally, we can consider enrichment with further structures, such
as distributive lattices and dcpos, and the language features these correspond to, such as
nondeterministic choice between programs
P\lor C
. These features are particularly interesting
from the perspective of programs as
specifications
, as they offer the potential for
representing complex specifications within SSA.

7.4.3.
Guarded Iteration

Elgot categories and monads were introduced in
Elgot (1975)
, and subsequently formalized
in
Adámek et al. (2011)
.
Levy and Goncharov (2019)
generalize Elgot
categories to
guarded Elgot categories
(and, correspondingly,
guarded Elgot monads
,
whose Kleisli categories are guarded Elgot), to support partial non-unique iteration. Generalizing
our language with support for guarded iteration could allow us to study applications of SSA to
domains for which not all fixpoints exist, such as the representation of terminating languages (as,
for example, one would find in a proof assistant) or productive processes. Another potential
advantage of such support is that every category with coproducts can be considered
vacuously

guarded, with the only fixpoints supported being those of loops which immediately exit. This could
allow us to unify the notion of
\lambda_{\mathsf{SSA}}
expression models and
\lambda_{\mathsf{SSA}}
models into the
single notion of
guarded

\lambda_{\mathsf{SSA}}
models (with expression models vacuously guarded,
while
\lambda_{\mathsf{SSA}}
models in the original sense would have all fixpoints).

Goncharov et al. (2021)
introduce a metalanguage for guarded iteration based on labelled
iteration as presented by
Geron and Levy (2016)
. Interestingly, they refer to the labels in
their systems as
exceptions
, making the claim that this more closely matches their semantics.
Similar to our work, they provide a denotational semantics for this language using the Kleisli
category of a strong guarded
pre-iterative
monad
\mathbf{T}
—that is, a monad with a guarded
fixpoint operator—on a distributive category
\mathcal{C}
. They then demonstrate that this semantics is
sound and adequate with respect to a big-step operational semantics for a specific monad, namely

\mathsf{T};X=X\times\mathbb{N}^{+}\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
over
\mathsf{Set}
. Notably, unlike our trace
monad, their monad is
guarded iterative
rather than merely iterative. The infinite branch of
the coproduct
\mathbb{N}^{\omega}
represents an infinite sequence of natural numbers, effectively
prohibiting non-productive infinite loops — that is, recursion that does not pass through a

guard
, which in this context is emitting a natural number. Their treatment of iteration is
thus somewhat more general than ours, as they support guarded iteration and require only a
pre-iterative monad. However, they focus on Kleisli categories rather than providing a general
treatment for Freyd categories. Additionally, their language supports functional types, whereas our
language is purely first-order. We are very interested in exploring the connection between guarded
labelled-iteration and SSA implied by the similarities between our respective syntaxes and
semantics.

Appendix A
The Environment Comonad

If
\mathcal{C}
is a Freyd category, then given an object
R\in|\mathcal{C}|
, the functor
R\otimes\cdot
is a comonad with counit
\pi_{r}:R\otimes A\to A
and comultiplication
{\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha:R\otimes A\to R\otimes(R\otimes A)
, often called the
environment comonad
or

coreader comonad
. In
\mathsf{Set}
, the co-Kleisli category of
R\otimes\cdot
is isomorphic
to the Kleisli category of the reader monad
R\to\cdot
, and thus can be equipped with the
structure of a distributive Elgot category. We might therefore intuit that, in general, if
\mathcal{C}

is a distributive Elgot category, the co-Kleisli category of
R\otimes\cdot
has this structure,
even if e.g.
\mathcal{C}
lacks exponentials. The rest of this section is dedicated to proving this,
which, beyond providing yet another class of
\lambda_{\mathsf{SSA}}
models, will also give us a some useful
equations and notation to use in our proofs later in the appendix.
We begin by giving an explicit definition:

Definition A.1 (Environment Comonad).

Given a Freyd category
\mathcal{C}
and an object (the
environment
)
R\in|\mathcal{C}|
, the

environment comonad

{\mathcal{C}}_{R\otimes\cdot}
is given by the functor
A\mapsto R\otimes A

and has counit
\pi_{r}
and comultiplication
\Delta_{R}\otimes A
. It follows that composition of
co-Kleisli morphisms
f:R\otimes A\to B
,
g:R\otimes B\to C
is given by

(112)

f\gg_{R}g:={\Delta}_{R};\alpha;R\otimes f;g:R\otimes A\to C

In particular, we can define an identity on objects functor
\mathsf{env}_{R}:\mathcal{C}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{R\otimes\cdot}
as follows:

(113)

\mathsf{env}_{R}(f):=\pi_{r};f

Where it is clear from context, we will leave out the environment
R
.

Given
f:R\otimes A\to B
, we will introduce the syntax sugar

(114)

\mathsf{rlet}(f):={\Delta}_{R};\alpha;R\otimes f:R\otimes A\to R\otimes B

In particular, we have that
f\gg g=\mathsf{rlet}(f);g
and
\mathsf{rlet}(\mathsf{env}(f)):=R\otimes f
. It is trivial to verify (for example, by drawing string diagrams) that

(115)

\begin{gathered}\mathsf{rlet}(\mathsf{rlet}(f);g)=\mathsf{rlet}(f);\mathsf{rlet}(g)\implies f\gg(g\gg h)=(f\gg g)\gg h\\
\mathsf{rlet}(f);\pi_{r}=f\implies f\gg\pi_{r}=f\qquad\mathsf{rlet}(\pi_{r})=\mathsf{id}\implies\pi_{r}\gg f=f\end{gathered}

and hence that
{\mathcal{C}}_{R\otimes\cdot}
is indeed a category. To show it is in fact a
Freyd
category
, we can define tensor functors

(116)

\displaystyle{f}\otimes_{R}{X}

\displaystyle:=\alpha^{-1};f\otimes X:R\otimes(A\otimes X)\to B\otimes X

\displaystyle{X}\otimes_{R}{f}

\displaystyle:=R\otimes\sigma;{f}\otimes_{R}{X};\sigma:R\otimes(X\otimes A)\to X\otimes B

We can define

•

Associators
\alpha^{R}_{A,B,C}:=\mathsf{env}_{R}(\alpha_{A,B,C})

•

Unitors
\lambda^{R}_{A}:=\mathsf{env}_{R}(\lambda_{A})
and
\rho^{R}_{A}:=\mathsf{env}_{R}(\rho_{A})

•

Symmetries
\sigma^{R}_{A,B}:=\mathsf{env}_{R}(\sigma_{A,B})

•

Projections
\pi_{l}^{R}:=\mathsf{env}_{R}(\pi_{l})
and
\pi_{r}^{R}:=\mathsf{env}_{R}(\pi_{r})

•

Terminal morphisms
!^{R}_{A}:=\mathsf{env}_{R}(!_{A})=!_{R\otimes A}

•

Diagonals
\Delta^{R}_{A}:=\mathsf{env}_{R}(\Delta_{A})

•

Pure morphisms
{{\mathcal{C}}_{R\otimes\cdot}}_{\bot}(A,B)=\mathcal{C}_{\bot}(R\otimes A,B)

Lemma A.2.

We can always equip
{\mathcal{C}}_{R\otimes\cdot}
with the structure of a Freyd category as described above
; furthermore,
\mathsf{env}_{R}(\cdot)
strictly preserves premonoidal structure.

Proof.

We have that

(117)

\displaystyle\mathsf{env}_{R}(f\otimes A)

\displaystyle=\pi_{r};f\otimes A=\alpha^{-1};(\pi_{r};f)\otimes A=\mathsf{env}_{R}(f)\otimes_{R}A

\displaystyle\mathsf{env}_{R}(A\otimes f)

\displaystyle=\pi_{r};A\otimes f=R\otimes\sigma;\alpha^{-1};(\pi_{r};f)\otimes A;\sigma=A\otimes_{R}\mathsf{env}_{R}(f)

and hence that
\mathsf{env}_{R}
preserves products. Since
\mathsf{env}_{R}
is a functor, to check we
indeed have a Freyd category, it suffices to show that:

•

\alpha^{R}_{A,B,C}
is natural: we have that, given

f:R\otimes A\to A^{\prime}
,
g:R\otimes B\to B^{\prime}
,
h:R\otimes C\to C^{\prime}

(118)

\displaystyle(f\otimes_{R}B)\otimes_{R}C\gg\alpha_{A^{\prime},B,C}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{A,B,C}\gg f\otimes_{R}(B\otimes C)

(119)

\displaystyle(A\otimes_{R}g)\otimes C\gg\alpha_{A,B^{\prime},C}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{A,B,C}\gg A\otimes_{R}(g\otimes_{R}C)

(120)

\displaystyle(A\otimes B)\otimes_{R}h\gg\alpha_{A,B,C^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{A,B,C}\gg A\otimes_{R}(B\otimes_{R}h)

since, for each equation, both sides are equal to the same string diagram in
Figure
36(a)
,
36(b)
, and
36(c)

respectively.

•

\lambda^{R}_{A}
is natural: we have that, for
f:R\otimes A\to A^{\prime}
,

(122)

\displaystyle f\otimes_{R}\mathbf{1}\gg\lambda^{R}_{A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R};\alpha;R\otimes(\alpha^{-1};f\otimes\mathbf{1});\pi_{r};\pi_{l}

\displaystyle=R\otimes\pi_{l};f

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R};\alpha;R\otimes(\pi_{r};\pi_{l});f

\displaystyle=\lambda^{R}_{A}\gg f

In general, we note that, for all
g:R\otimes A\to A^{\prime}\otimes\mathbf{1}
,

g\gg\lambda^{R}=g;\pi_{l}
.

•

\rho^{R}_{A}
is natural:

(123)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\otimes_{R}f\gg\rho^{R}_{A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes(\mathbf{1}\otimes A);\alpha;R\otimes(R\otimes\sigma;\alpha^{-1};f\otimes\mathbf{1};\sigma);\pi_{r};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=R\otimes\pi_{r};f

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R};\alpha;R\otimes(\pi_{r};\pi_{r});f

\displaystyle=\rho^{R}_{A}\gg f

In general, we note that, for all
g:R\otimes A\to\mathbf{1}\otimes A^{\prime}
,

g\gg\rho^{R}=g;\pi_{r}
.

•

\sigma^{R}_{A,B}
is natural: given
f:R\otimes A\to A^{\prime}
and
g:R\otimes B\to B^{\prime}
,
we have that

(124)

\displaystyle f\otimes_{R}B\gg\sigma^{R}_{A^{\prime},B}

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes(A\otimes B);\alpha;R\otimes(\alpha^{-1};f\otimes B);\pi_{r};\sigma

\displaystyle=\alpha^{-1};f\otimes B;\sigma

\displaystyle=R\otimes\sigma;R\otimes\sigma;f\otimes B;\sigma

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes(B\otimes A);R\otimes(\pi_{r};\sigma);R\otimes\sigma;\alpha^{-1};f\otimes B;\sigma

\displaystyle=\sigma^{R}_{A,B}\gg B\otimes_{R}f

and

(125)

\displaystyle A\otimes g\gg\sigma^{R}_{A,B^{\prime}}

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes(A\otimes B);\alpha;R\otimes(R\otimes\sigma;\alpha^{-1};g\otimes A;\sigma);\pi_{r};\sigma

\displaystyle=R\otimes\sigma;\alpha^{-1};g\otimes A;\cancel{\sigma;\sigma}

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes(A\otimes B);\alpha;R\otimes(\pi_{r};\sigma);\alpha^{-1};g\otimes A

\displaystyle=\sigma^{R}_{A,B}\gg g\otimes_{R}A

•

Pure morphisms are central: given
f:R\otimes A\to A^{\prime}
and
g:R\otimes B\to B^{\prime}

pure, we have that

(126)

\displaystyle f\otimes_{R}B\gg A^{\prime}\otimes_{R}g

\displaystyle=\Delta_{R};\alpha;R\otimes(\alpha^{-1};f\otimes B;\sigma);\alpha^{-1};g\otimes A^{\prime};\sigma

\displaystyle=\Delta_{R};\alpha;R\otimes(R\otimes\sigma;\alpha^{-1};g\otimes A;\sigma);\alpha^{-1};f\otimes B^{\prime}

\displaystyle=A\otimes_{R}g\gg f\otimes_{R}B^{\prime}

since both sides correspond to the diagram in Figure
37
. We will write this as
f\otimes_{R}g
.

•

!^{R}_{A}
is terminal for pure morphisms: yes, since
!^{R}_{A}
is just
!_{R\otimes A}
which
is terminal in
\mathcal{C}

•

\Delta^{R}_{A}
duplicates pure morphisms: we have

(127)

\displaystyle f\gg\Delta^{R}_{B}

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R};\alpha;R\otimes f;\pi_{r};{\Delta}_{A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=f;{\Delta}_{A^{\prime}}={\Delta}_{R\otimes A};f\otimes f

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R};\alpha;R\otimes(\alpha^{-1};f\otimes A^{\prime});R\otimes\sigma;\alpha^{-1};f\otimes A^{\prime};\sigma

\displaystyle=\Delta^{R}_{A}\gg f\otimes_{R}f

∎

.
Centrality of pure morphisms in the environment comonad’s co-Kleisli category

Now, assume
\mathcal{C}
is distributive. We wish to show that
A+B
is a coproduct in

{\mathcal{C}}_{R\otimes\cdot}
, and furthermore that
{\mathcal{C}}_{R\otimes\cdot}
is distributive; note that even the
former may not be the case without distributivity! We proceed as follows:

Lemma A.3.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a distributive Freyd category, then
{\mathcal{C}}_{R\otimes\cdot}
is also distributive Freyd

Proof.

Given
f:R\otimes A\to B
and
g:R\otimes A\to C
, we may define the coproduct and
injections

(128)

[f,g]_{R}:=\delta^{-1};[f,g]:R\otimes A\to B+C\qquad\iota^{R}_{l}:=\pi_{r};\iota_{r}:R\otimes A\to A+B\qquad\iota^{R}_{r}:=\pi_{r};\iota_{r}:R\otimes B\to A+B

To verify this is indeed a coproduct, we can check that

(129)

\displaystyle\iota^{R}_{l}\gg[f,g]_{R}

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R};\alpha;R\otimes(\pi_{r};\iota_{r});\delta^{-1};[f,g]=R\otimes\iota_{r};\delta^{-1};[f,g]=f

\displaystyle\iota^{R}_{r}\gg[f,g]_{R}

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R};\alpha;R\otimes(\pi_{r};\iota_{l});\delta^{-1};[f,g]=R\otimes\iota_{l};\delta^{-1};[f,g]=g

This morphism is obviously unique, since we have for all
h:R\otimes(A+B)\to C

(130)

[\iota^{R}_{l}\gg h,\iota^{R}_{r}\gg h]_{R}=\delta^{-1};[{\Delta}_{R};\alpha;R\otimes\iota_{l};\pi_{r};h,{\Delta}_{R};\alpha;R\otimes\iota_{r};\pi_{r};h]=\cancel{\delta^{-1};[R\otimes\iota_{r},R\otimes\iota_{l}]};h

To show that
{R}_{\mathcal{C}\otimes\cdot}
is indeed distributive, we need
\delta^{R-1}:=\mathsf{env}_{R}(\delta^{-1})=\pi_{r};\delta^{R}
to be the inverse to
\delta^{R}:=[A\otimes\iota^{R}_{l},A\otimes\iota^{R}_{r}]_{R}
, which can easily be derived from the functoriality of

\mathsf{env}_{R}(\cdot)
, since

(131)

\mathsf{env}_{R}(\delta)=\pi_{r};[\iota_{l};\iota_{r}]=\delta^{-1};[\pi_{r};\iota_{l},\pi_{r}\iota_{r}]=\delta^{R}

∎

Note in particular that this allows us to define sums

(132)

\displaystyle f+_{R}g

\displaystyle=[f\gg\iota^{R}_{l},g\gg\iota^{R}_{r}]_{R}

\displaystyle=\delta^{-1};[{\Delta}_{R}\otimes(A+B);\alpha;R\otimes f;\pi_{r};\iota_{l},{\Delta}_{R}\otimes(A+B);\alpha;R\otimes g;\pi_{r};\iota_{r}]

\displaystyle=\delta^{-1};f+g

Our final task is to show that, if
\mathcal{C}
is a strong Elgot category, then so is

{R}_{\mathcal{C}\otimes\cdot}
, and, in particular, with fixpoint operator
\mathsf{rfix}(f)
. Note that, just like we
needed distributivity to have coproducts at all, we will need strength to have an Elgot structure.
We begin by stating some generally useful properties of
\mathsf{rcase}(f)
and
\mathsf{rfix}(f)
. In
particular, we have that: For
f:R\otimes A\to B+C
:

•

Given
h:R\otimes X\to A
, we have

(133)

\displaystyle\mathsf{rlet}(h);\mathsf{rcase}(f)

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes X;\alpha;R\otimes h;{\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1}

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes X;\alpha;R\otimes({\Delta}_{R}\otimes X;\alpha;R\otimes h;f);\delta^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rcase}(\mathsf{rlet}(h);f)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rcase}(h\gg f)

•

Given
g:R\otimes B\to X
,
h:R\otimes C\to Y
, we have

(134)

\displaystyle\mathsf{rcase}(f\gg g+_{R}h)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rlet}(f\gg g+_{R}h);\delta^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rlet}(f);\mathsf{rlet}(g+_{R}h);\delta^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rlet}(f);{\Delta}_{R}\otimes(B+C);\alpha;R\otimes(\delta^{-1};g+h);\delta^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rlet}(f);{\Delta}_{R}\otimes(B+C);\alpha;R\otimes\delta^{-1};\delta^{-1};(R\otimes g)+(R\otimes h)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rlet}(f);\delta^{-1};\mathsf{rlet}(g)+\mathsf{rlet}(h)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rcase}(f);\mathsf{rlet}(g)+\mathsf{rlet}(h)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rlet}(f);\mathsf{rlet}(g)+_{R}\mathsf{rlet}(h)

Similarly, for
f:R\otimes A\to B+A
:

•

Given
h:R^{\prime}\to_{\bot}R
,
h\otimes A;\mathsf{rfix}(f)=\mathsf{rfix}(h\otimes A;f)
: we have that

(135)

\displaystyle h\otimes A;\mathsf{rcase}(f)

\displaystyle=h\otimes A;{\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1}

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R^{\prime}}\otimes A;\alpha;h\otimes(h\otimes A;f);\delta^{-1}

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R^{\prime}}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes(h\otimes A;f);\delta^{-1};h\otimes B+h\otimes A

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rcase}(h\otimes A;f);h\otimes B+h\otimes A

and hence by uniformity that

(136)

\displaystyle h\otimes A;\mathsf{rfix}(f)

\displaystyle=h\otimes A;(\mathsf{rcase}(f))^{\dagger};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{rcase}(h\otimes A;f);h\otimes B+R^{\prime}\otimes A)^{\dagger};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{rcase}(h\otimes A;f))^{\dagger};h\otimes B;\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rfix}(h\otimes A;f)

•

\mathsf{rlet}(\mathsf{rfix}(f))=(\mathsf{rcase}(f))^{\dagger}
: we have that

(137)

\displaystyle({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha);(R\otimes\mathsf{rcase}(f);\delta^{-1};R\otimes\pi_{r}+R\otimes(R\otimes A))

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes(\mathsf{let}(f);\pi_{l}\otimes(B+A);\delta^{-1});\delta^{-1};R\otimes\pi_{r}+R\otimes(R\otimes A)

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;{\Delta}_{R}\otimes(B+A);\alpha;R\otimes\delta^{-1};\delta^{-1};R\otimes\pi_{r}+R\otimes(R\otimes A)

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes\pi_{r})+({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha)

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R\otimes A};(R\otimes A)\otimes f;\pi_{l}\otimes(B+A);\delta^{-1};R\otimes A+({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rcase}(f);R\otimes A+({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha)

It follows by uniformity and strength that

(138)

\displaystyle\mathsf{rlet}(\mathsf{rfix}(f))=\mathsf{let}(\mathsf{rfix}(f));\pi_{l}\otimes B

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R\otimes A};(R\otimes A)\otimes\mathsf{rfix}(f);\pi_{l}\otimes B

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes\mathsf{rfix}(f)

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes((\mathsf{rcase}(f))^{\dagger};\pi_{r})

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;(R\otimes\mathsf{rcase}(f);\delta^{-1};R\otimes\pi_{r}+R\otimes(R\otimes A))^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{rcase}(f))^{\dagger}

as desired.

We also state some generally useful properties of
\mathsf{rlet}(f)
:

•

For all
f:R\otimes A\to C
,
g:R\otimes B\to C
,

(139)

\displaystyle\mathsf{rlet}(\delta^{-1};[f,g])

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes(A+B);\alpha;R\otimes(\delta^{-1};[f,g])

\displaystyle=\delta^{-1};[{\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;f,{\Delta}_{R}\otimes B;\alpha;g]

\displaystyle=\delta^{-1};[\mathsf{rlet}(f),\mathsf{rlet}(g)]

We may now state our desired result as follows:

Lemma A.4.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a premonoidal strong Elgot category, then so is
{\mathcal{C}}_{R\otimes\cdot}
with
fixpoint operator
\mathsf{rfix}(f)
.

Proof.

We check each of the Elgot axioms as follows:

•

Fixpoint:
given
f:R\otimes A\to B+A
, we have that

(140)

\displaystyle\mathsf{rfix}(f)

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{rcase}(f))^{\dagger};\pi_{r}=(\mathsf{rlet}(f);\delta^{-1})^{\dagger};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rlet}(f);\delta^{-1};[\mathsf{id}_{R\otimes B},(\mathsf{rlet}(f);\delta^{-1})^{\dagger}];\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rlet}(f);\delta^{-1};[\pi_{r},(\mathsf{rcase}(f))^{\dagger};\pi_{r}]

\displaystyle=f\gg[\pi_{r},\mathsf{rfix}(f)]_{R}

as desired.

•

Naturality:
given
f:R\otimes A\to B+A
,
g:R\otimes B\to C
, we have that

(141)

\displaystyle\mathsf{rfix}(f\gg g+_{R}A)

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{rlet}({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};g+\pi_{r});\delta^{-1})^{\dagger};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{rlet}({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1});\delta^{-1};R\otimes g+R\otimes\pi_{r})^{\dagger};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1});\delta^{-1};(\pi_{r};g)+R\otimes\pi_{r})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};({\Delta}_{R}\otimes B;\alpha;\pi_{r};g)+({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes\pi_{r}))^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};g+R\otimes A)^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1})^{\dagger};g=(\mathsf{rcase}(f))^{\dagger};g

On the other hand, we have that

(142)

\displaystyle\mathsf{rfix}(f)\gg g

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes(\mathsf{rcase}(f);\pi_{r}+R\otimes A)^{\dagger};g

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;(R\otimes(\mathsf{rcase}(f);\pi_{r}+R\otimes A);\delta^{-1})^{\dagger};g

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;(R\otimes({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};\pi_{r}+R\otimes A);\delta^{-1})^{\dagger};g

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;(R\otimes({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1});\delta^{-1};R\otimes\pi_{r}+R\otimes(R\otimes A))^{\dagger};g

By uniformity, it hence suffices to show that

(143)

\displaystyle({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha);(R\otimes({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1});\delta^{-1};R\otimes\pi_{r}+R\otimes(R\otimes A))

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};({\Delta}_{R}\otimes B;\alpha;R\otimes\pi_{r})+({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rcase}(f);(R\otimes B)+({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha)

to yield the desired result.

•

Codiagonal:
given
f:R\otimes A\to(B+A)+A
, we have

(144)

\displaystyle\mathsf{rfix}(\mathsf{rfix}(f))

\displaystyle=({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha\ ;R\otimes((\mathsf{rcase}(f))^{\dagger};\pi_{r});\delta^{-1})^{\dagger};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes(\mathsf{rcase}(f);\pi_{r}+R\otimes A)^{\dagger};\delta^{-1})^{\dagger};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;(R\otimes(\mathsf{rcase}(f);\pi_{r}+R\otimes A);\delta^{-1})^{\dagger};\delta^{-1})^{\dagger};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;(R\otimes(\mathsf{rcase}(f);\pi_{r}+R\otimes A);\delta^{-1};\delta^{-1}+R\otimes(R\otimes A))^{\dagger})^{\dagger};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;(R\otimes(\mathsf{rcase}(f);\pi_{r}+R\otimes A);\delta^{-1};\delta^{-1}+R\otimes(R\otimes A))^{\dagger};\pi_{r}+R\otimes A)^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;(R\otimes(\mathsf{rcase}(f);\pi_{r}+R\otimes A);\delta^{-1};(\delta^{-1};(\pi_{r}+R\otimes A))+R\otimes(R\otimes A))^{\dagger})^{\dagger}

On the other hand, we have

(145)

\displaystyle\mathsf{rfix}(f\gg[\pi_{r},\iota^{R}_{l}]_{R})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rfix}({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};[\pi_{r},\pi_{r};\iota_{l}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rfix}(f;[\mathsf{id},\iota_{l}])

\displaystyle=({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes(f;[\mathsf{id},\iota_{l}]);\delta^{-1})^{\dagger};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};\delta^{-1}+R\otimes A;[\mathsf{id},\iota_{l}])^{\dagger};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=(({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};\delta^{-1}+R\otimes A)^{\dagger})^{\dagger};\pi_{r}

\displaystyle=(({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};\delta^{-1}+R\otimes A)^{\dagger};\pi_{r}+R\otimes A)^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=(({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};\delta^{-1}+R\otimes A;(\pi_{r}+R\otimes A)+R\otimes A)^{\dagger})^{\dagger}

By uniformity, it therefore suffices to show that

(146)

\displaystyle({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha);(R\otimes(\mathsf{rcase}(f);\pi_{r}+R\otimes A);\delta^{-1};(\delta^{-1};(\pi_{r}+R\otimes A))+R\otimes(R\otimes A))

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};\pi_{r}+R\otimes A);\delta^{-1};(\delta^{-1};(\pi_{r}+R\otimes A))+R\otimes(R\otimes A)

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1});

\displaystyle\delta^{-1};R\otimes\pi_{r}+R\otimes(R\otimes A);(\delta^{-1};(\pi_{r}+R\otimes A))+R\otimes(R\otimes A)

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha;R\otimes f;\delta^{-1};\delta^{-1}+(R\otimes A);(\pi_{r}+R\otimes A)+({\Delta}_{R}\otimes A;\alpha)

to obtain the desired result.

•

Uniformity:

Given
f:R\otimes A\to B+A
,
g:R\otimes X\to B+X
and
h:R\otimes X\to_{\bot}A

such that
h\gg f=g\gg B+_{R}h
, we have that

(147)

\displaystyle\mathsf{rlet}(h);\mathsf{rcase}(f)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rcase}(h\gg f)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rcase}(g\gg B+_{R}h)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rcase}(g);(R\otimes B)+\mathsf{rlet}(h)

and hence by uniformity that

(148)

h\gg\mathsf{rfix}(f)=\mathsf{rlet}(h);(\mathsf{rcase}(f))^{\dagger};\pi_{r}=(\mathsf{rcase}(g))^{\dagger};\pi_{r}=\mathsf{rfix}(g)

∎

Appendix B
Proofs

B.1.
Strict SSA

\anfconversion

*

\ssaconversion

*

Proof.

We may show that
\mathsf{ANF}(r)
is always in ANF and that
\mathsf{ANF}_{\mathsf{let}}(x,a,r)
is in ANF
if
r
is by a straightforward induction. To prove the rest of the lemma, we begin proving the
correctness of
\mathsf{ANF}_{\mathsf{let}}(x,a,r)
by induction on expressions
a
:

•

If
\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}^{\mathsf{anf}}a:{A}
is atomic, we are done; otherwise

•

If
a=f\;e
, then, since
e
is a subterm of
a
, by induction, we have

(149)

\mathsf{let}\;x=a;r\approx\mathsf{let}\;y=e;\mathsf{let}\;x=f\;y;r\approx\mathsf{ANF}_{\mathsf{let}}(y,e,\mathsf{let}\;x=f\;y;r)=\mathsf{ANF}_{\mathsf{let}}(x,a,r)

as desired.

•

The cases for pairs, injections, and aborts containing expressions are analogous

•

If
a=\mathsf{case}\;e\;\{\iota_{l}\;{y}:b,\iota_{r}\;{z}:c\}
, then we may rewrite

(150)

\mathsf{let}\;x=a;r\approx\mathsf{let}\;w=e;\mathsf{case}\;w\;\{\iota_{l}\;{y}:\mathsf{let}\;b=x;\;r,\iota_{r}\;{z}:\mathsf{let}\;c=x;\;r\}

Since
r
is in ANF, by induction (since
b,c
are subterms of
a
), this is equivalent to

(151)

\mathsf{let}\;w=e;\mathsf{case}\;w\;\{\iota_{l}\;{y}:\mathsf{ANF}_{\mathsf{let}}(b,x,r),\iota_{r}\;{z}:\mathsf{ANF}_{\mathsf{let}}(c,x,r)\}

which is equal to
\mathsf{ANF}_{\mathsf{let}}(x,a,r)
, as desired.

•

The cases for unary and binary
\mathsf{let}
are analogous to the above

We may now prove the correctness of
\mathsf{ANF}(r)
by a straightforward induction on
r
:

•

If
r=\mathsf{br}\;\ell\;a
, by
\beta
-reduction, we have that

(152)

r\approx\mathsf{let}\;x=a;\;\mathsf{br}\;\ell\;x\approx\mathsf{ANF}_{\mathsf{let}}(x,a,\mathsf{br}\;\ell\;x)=\mathsf{ANF}(r)

•

If
r=\mathsf{let}\;x=a;r^{\prime}
, then by induction we have that

(153)

r\approx\mathsf{let}\;x=a;\mathsf{ANF}(r^{\prime})\approx\mathsf{ANF}_{\mathsf{let}}(x,a,\mathsf{ANF}(r^{\prime}))=\mathsf{ANF}(r)

as desired.

•

If
r=\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=a;r^{\prime}
, then by induction we have that

(154)

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle\approx\mathsf{let}\;z=a;\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=z;r^{\prime}

\displaystyle\approx\mathsf{let}\;z=a;\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=z;\mathsf{ANF}(r^{\prime})

\displaystyle\approx\mathsf{ANF}_{\mathsf{let}}(z,a,\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=z;\mathsf{ANF}(r^{\prime}))\approx\mathsf{ANF}(r)

The proof for
\mathsf{case}
-statements is analogous

•

The case for control-flow graphs follows trivially by induction

∎

Proof.

Given that
\mathsf{SSA}_{\mathsf{a}}(r,G)\approx r\;\mathsf{where}\;G
for
r
in ANF, it is trivial to see
that

(155)

\mathsf{SSA}(r):=\mathsf{SSA}_{\mathsf{a}}(\mathsf{ANF}(r),\cdot)\approx(\mathsf{ANF}(r)\;\mathsf{where}\;\cdot)\approx\mathsf{ANF}(r)\approx r

We hence only need to prove the second part of the lemma. We proceed by induction on ANF regions

r
as follows:

•

If
r
is a terminator, this holds trivially by reflexivity

•

If
r=\mathsf{let}\;x=a;r^{\prime}
, then by induction, we have that

(156)

\mathsf{SSA}_{\mathsf{a}}(r,G):=\mathsf{let}\;x=a;\mathsf{SSA}_{\mathsf{a}}(r^{\prime},G)\approx\mathsf{let}\;x=a;r^{\prime}\;\mathsf{where}\;G\approx\mathsf{let}\;x=a;r^{\prime}\;\mathsf{where}\;G\approx r\;\mathsf{where}\;G

as desired. The case for binary
\mathsf{let}
-statements is analogous.

•

If
r=\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:s,\iota_{r}\;{y}:t\}
, then by induction, we have that

(157)

\displaystyle\mathsf{SSA}_{\mathsf{a}}(r,G)

\displaystyle:=(\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:\mathsf{br}\;\ell_{l}\;x,\iota_{r}\;{y}:\mathsf{br}\;\ell_{r}\;y\})\;\mathsf{where}\;G,\ell_{l}(x):\{\mathsf{SSA}(s)\},\ell_{r}(y):\{\mathsf{SSA}(t)\}

\displaystyle:=(\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:\mathsf{br}\;\ell_{l}\;x,\iota_{r}\;{y}:\mathsf{br}\;\ell_{r}\;y\})\;\mathsf{where}\;G,\ell_{l}(x):\{s\},\ell_{r}(y):\{t\}

\displaystyle\approx((\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:\mathsf{br}\;\ell_{l}\;x,\iota_{r}\;{y}:\mathsf{br}\;\ell_{r}\;y\})\;\mathsf{where}\;\ell_{l}(x):\{s\},\ell_{r}(y):\{t\})\;\mathsf{where}\;G

\displaystyle\approx\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:s,\iota_{r}\;{y}:t\}\;\mathsf{where}\;G\approx r\;\mathsf{where}\;G

•

If
r=r^{\prime}\;\mathsf{where}\;\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}
, then by induction, we have that

(158)

\displaystyle\mathsf{SSA}_{\mathsf{a}}(r,G)

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{SSA}_{\mathsf{a}}(r^{\prime},G,(\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{\mathsf{SSA}(t_{i})\},)_{i})

\displaystyle\approx r^{\prime}\;\mathsf{where}\;G,(\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}

\displaystyle\approx(r^{\prime}\;\mathsf{where}\;(\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}\;\mathsf{where}\;G\approx r\;\mathsf{where}\;G

∎

\cfgperminvar

*

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the size of
G
. If
G
consists only of an entry block, there is only
one permutation, so we are done. Otherwise, assume
G=\beta,(\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}
.
Furthermore, let:

•

\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{\beta_{i}\}
be the children of
\beta
in
G
in order

•

G_{i}=(\kappa_{i,j}(y_{i,j}):\{t_{i,j}\},)_{j}
be the CFG composed of the
descendants of
\beta_{i}
in
G
, with
\beta_{i}
as entry block, in order

•

\ell_{i}^{\prime}(x_{i}^{\prime}):\{\beta_{i}^{\prime}\}
be the children of
\beta
in
G^{\prime}
in order

•

G_{i}^{\prime}
be the CFG composed of the descendants of
\beta_{i}^{\prime}
in
G^{\prime}
, with
\beta_{i}^{\prime}
as
entry block, in order

By assumption, there exists some permutation
\sigma
such that
G^{\prime}=\beta,(\ell_{\sigma_{i}}(x_{\sigma_{i}}):\{t_{\sigma_{i}}\},)_{i}
. It follows that, since the dominance
relation on labels is permutation-invariant, there exists some permutation
\rho
such that

\ell_{i}^{\prime}(x_{i}^{\prime}):\{\beta_{i}^{\prime}\}=\ell_{\rho_{i}}(x_{\rho_{i}}):\{\beta_{\rho_{i}}\}
, as well
as permutations
\tau_{i}
such that
G_{i}^{\prime}=(\ell_{\rho_{i},\tau_{ij}}(x_{\rho_{i},\tau_{ij}}):\{t_{\rho_{i},\tau_{ij}}\},)_{j}
, implying in particular that
G_{i}^{\prime}\simeq G_{\rho_{i}}
. By
induction, we hence have that, for all
i
,
\mathsf{reg}(G_{i}^{\prime})\approx\mathsf{reg}(G_{\rho_{i}})
, and hence
that

(159)

\displaystyle\mathsf{reg}(G^{\prime})

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{bb}(\beta,(\ell_{\rho_{i}}(x_{\rho_{i}}):\{\mathsf{reg}(G_{i}^{\prime})\},)_{i})

\displaystyle\approx\mathsf{bb}(\beta,(\ell_{\rho_{i}}(x_{\rho_{i}}):\{\mathsf{reg}(G_{\rho_{i}})\},)_{i})

\displaystyle\approx\mathsf{bb}(\beta,(\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{\mathsf{reg}(G_{i})\},)_{i})\approx\mathsf{reg}(G)

∎

\cfgconversion

*

Proof.

We will proceed by induction on the length of
G=\mathsf{cfg}(r)
. If
G
consists of only an entry
block
\beta
, then we trivially have that
r=\mathsf{reg}(\mathsf{cfg}(r))
, and so we are done. Otherwise,
assume
r=\mathsf{bb}(\beta,(\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i})
. Clearly,
\mathsf{cfg}(r)
will have entry
block
\beta
; moreover, since every block in
\mathsf{cfg}(r)
other than those of the form

\mathsf{entry}(t_{i})
can only be reached from within the region
t_{i}
(due to lexical scoping of
labels), we have
\beta_{i}=\mathsf{entry}(t_{\rho_{i}})
for some injection
\rho
, where
\beta_{i}
are
the children of
\beta
in the dominance tree of
G
. In particular, we can write

\{\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{\mathsf{entry}(t_{i})\},\}_{i}
as the disjoint union of:

•

The immediate children of
\beta
,
\kappa_{i}(y_{i}):\{\beta_{i}\}
, where

\kappa_{i}=\ell_{\rho_{i}}
,
y_{i}=x_{\rho_{i}}
, and
\beta_{i}=\mathsf{entry}(t_{\rho_{i}})

•

The nodes dominated by each
\beta_{i}
but not immediately dominated by
\beta
; we will
write the collection of such nodes for each
i
as
\kappa_{i,j}(y_{i,j}):\{\beta_{i,j}\}
, where
\kappa_{i,j}=\ell_{\rho_{i,j}}
,
y_{i,j}=x_{\rho_{i,j}}
, and
\beta_{i,j}=\mathsf{entry}(t_{\rho_{i,j}})
.

As in the algorithm to compute
\mathsf{reg}(\cdot)
, let
G_{i}
denote the control-flow graph with
entry block
\beta_{i}
consisting of all blocks dominated by
\beta_{i}
. We may write

(160)

G\simeq\beta,(\kappa_{i}(y_{i}):\{G_{i}\},)_{i}\qquad\forall i,G_{i}=\beta_{i},(\kappa_{i,j}(y_{i,j}):\{\beta_{i,j}\},)_{j},R_{i}

where
R_{i}
is the “remainder” of the control-flow graph
G_{i}
. Note the equations for
G_{i}
are
actually equalities, rather than being equivalence up to permutation
\simeq
, since the

\beta_{i,j}
, appear in
G
before any other elements, being immediate children of
\beta
. It
follows that

(161)

\mathsf{reg}(\mathsf{cfg}(r))=\mathsf{reg}(\mathsf{cfg}(\mathsf{bb}(\beta,(\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i})))=\mathsf{bb}(\beta,(\kappa_{i}(y_{i}):\{\mathsf{reg}(G_{i})\},)_{i})

Now, define the strict regions

(162)

r_{i}=\mathsf{bb}(\beta_{i},((\kappa_{i,j}(y_{i,j}):\{t_{\rho_{i,j}}\},)_{j},\mathsf{children}(t_{i})))

It is easy to show that
\mathsf{cfg}(r_{i})\simeq G_{i}
, since every basic block dominated by
\beta_{i}

must be dominated via either some
t_{\rho_{i,j}}
or some child of
t_{i}
. Since by induction

\mathsf{reg}(\mathsf{cfg}(r_{i}))\approx r_{i}
, and by the previous lemma
\mathsf{reg}(\mathsf{cfg}(_{)}r_{i})\approx\mathsf{reg}(G_{i})
,
it follows that

(163)

\mathsf{reg}(\mathsf{cfg}(r))\approx\mathsf{bb}(\beta,(\kappa_{i}(y_{i}):\{r_{i}\},)_{i})

Define

(164)

T_{i}=(\kappa_{j}(y_{j}):\{r_{j}\},)_{j<i},(\kappa_{j}(y_{j}):\{t_{j}\},(\kappa_{j,k}(y_{j,k}):\{t_{\rho_{j,k}}\},)_{k},)_{j\geq i}

Using Equation
27
, we may show that, for all
i
,
\mathsf{bb}(\beta,T_{i+1})\approx\mathsf{bb}(\beta,T_{i})
, since

•

For all
j
,
t_{\rho_{i,j}}
cannot use variables defined in
\beta_{i}
, or
r
would not
typecheck

•

For all
j
, if
r_{k}
or
t_{\rho_{k,j^{\prime}}}
calls
\kappa_{i,j}
, then
k=i
, or

\beta_{i,j}
would not be dominated by
\beta_{i}

•

Similarly,
\beta
cannot call
\kappa_{i,j}
or
\beta_{i,j}
would be a direct
descendant of
\beta

We hence have by induction that

(165)

\mathsf{reg}(\mathsf{cfg}(r))\approx\mathsf{bb}(\beta,T_{N})\approx\mathsf{bb}(\beta,T_{0})\approx r

as desired, since
T_{0}
is a permutation of
(\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}
.
∎

B.2.
Böhm-Jacopini

Lemma B.1.

The following facts hold:

•

Given
\Gamma\vdash r\rhd\blacksquare(A)
and
\Gamma,\square:A\vdash s\rhd\mathsf{L}
,
we have that

(166)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{seq}(r,s):=([\blacksquare(x)\mapsto\mathsf{br}\;\ell\;x]r\;\mathsf{where}\;\ell(\square):\{s\})\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash[\blacksquare(x)\mapsto\mathsf{br}\;\ell\;x]r\rhd\ell(A)}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{A};\llbracket{\Gamma,\square:A\vdash s\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash r\rhd\ell(A)}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{A};\llbracket{\Gamma,\square:A\vdash s\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket

•

Given
\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}e:{A}
,
\Gamma,\square:A\vdash r\rhd\blacksquare(B+A)
,
we have that

(167)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{loop}(e,r):=(\mathsf{br}\;\ell\;e\;\mathsf{where}\;\ell(\square):\{\mathsf{seq}(r,\mathsf{case}\;\square\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:\mathsf{br}\;\blacksquare\;x,\iota_{r}\;{y}:\mathsf{br}\;\ell\;y\})\})\rhd\blacksquare(B)}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}e:{A}}\rrbracket);\mathsf{rfix}(\llbracket{\Gamma,\square:A\vdash r\rhd\blacksquare(B+A)}\rrbracket;\alpha^{+}_{B+A})

•

Given
\mathsf{R}=(\ell_{i}(A_{i}),)_{i}
and
\Gamma,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash t_{i}\rhd\mathsf{L}
, we
have

(168)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma,c:\langle\mathsf{R}\rangle\vdash\mathsf{case}_{\mathsf{R}}\;c\;\{\ell_{i}(x_{i}):t_{i}\}\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\delta^{-1}_{\Sigma};[\llbracket{\Gamma,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash t_{i}\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket,]_{i}

We have that

(169)

\llbracket{\Gamma,\square:\bot\vdash_{[\mathsf{L}]}\mathsf{ua}\;\square:{\langle\mathsf{L}\rangle+\langle\mathsf{R}\rangle}}\rrbracket=\pi_{r};\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket}

We have that

(170)

\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{pack}_{\kappa}^{+}(\mathsf{L}):\mathsf{L}\rightsquigarrow\kappa(\langle\mathsf{L}\rangle)}\rrbracket=\pi_{r};\alpha^{+}_{\mathbf{0}+\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket}

and

(171)

\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{unpack}_{\kappa}^{+}(\mathsf{L}):\kappa(\langle\mathsf{L}\rangle)\rightsquigarrow\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket=\pi_{r};\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket}

In particular, given
\Gamma\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L}
, we have that

(172)

\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash\langle r\rangle^{+}\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L}\rangle)}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\alpha^{+}_{\mathbf{0}+\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket}

\bohmjacopini

*

Proof.

We begin by showing the correctness of
\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(r)
by induction on
r
:

•

If
r=\mathsf{br}\;\ell\;a
, we have that

\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(a)=\mathsf{pack}^{+}(\mathsf{L})_{\ell}(a)=[\mathsf{br}\;\ell\;a]^{+}

by definition

•

If
r=\mathsf{let}\;x=a;s
, we have by induction that

\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(\mathsf{let}\;x=a;s)=\mathsf{let}\;x=a;\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(s)\approx\mathsf{let}\;x=a;[s]^{+}=[\mathsf{let}\;x=a;s]^{+}

•

If
r=\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=a;s
, we have by induction that

\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=a;s)=\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=a;\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(s)\approx\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=a;\langle s\rangle^{+}=\langle\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=a;s\rangle^{+}

•

If
r=\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:s,\iota_{r}\;{y}:t\}
, we have by induction that

\displaystyle\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:s,\iota_{r}\;{y}:t\})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(s),\iota_{r}\;{y}:\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(t)\}

\displaystyle\approx\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:\langle s\rangle^{+},\iota_{r}\;{y}:\langle t\rangle^{+}\}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:s,\iota_{r}\;{y}:t\}\rangle^{+}

•

Assume
r=s\;\mathsf{where}\;(\ell_{i}(t_{i}):\{x_{i}\},)_{i}
. Define
\mathsf{R}=(\ell_{i}(A_{i}),)_{i}
.
By induction, we have that
\forall i,\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}(t_{i})\approx\langle t_{i}\rangle^{+}
, and
hence by soundness

(173)

\llbracket{\Gamma,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(t_{i})\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\rangle)}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\Gamma,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash\langle t_{i}\rangle^{+}\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\rangle)}\rrbracket

Now, define

D=\mathsf{case}_{\mathsf{L}}\;\square\;\{\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\mathsf{seq}(\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(t_{i}),\mathsf{br}\;\blacksquare\;(\mathsf{ua}\;\square)\})

and
L=\mathsf{loop}(y,D)
. It follows that

(174)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma,\square:[R]\vdash D\rhd\blacksquare(\langle L\rangle+\langle R\rangle)}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\delta^{-1}_{\Sigma};[\llbracket{\Gamma,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash\mathsf{seq}(\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(t_{i}),\mathsf{br}\;\blacksquare\;(\mathsf{ua}\;\square))\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L}\rangle+\langle\mathsf{R}\rangle)}\rrbracket,]_{i}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\delta^{-1}_{\Sigma};[\llbracket{\Gamma,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(t_{i})\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\rangle)}\rrbracket;\alpha^{+}_{\mathbf{0}+(\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket)},]_{i}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\delta^{-1}_{\Sigma};[\llbracket{\Gamma,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash\langle t_{i}\rangle^{+}\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\rangle)}\rrbracket;\alpha^{+}_{\mathbf{0}+(\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket)},]_{i}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\delta^{-1}_{\Sigma};[\llbracket{\Gamma,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash t_{i}\rhd\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\alpha^{+}_{\mathbf{0}+(\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket)},]_{i}

and therefore that

(175)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma,y:\langle R\rangle\vdash L\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L}\rangle)}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma,y:\langle R\rangle\vdash_{\bot}y:{A}}\rrbracket);\mathsf{rfix}(\llbracket{\Gamma,\square:\langle R\rangle\vdash D\rhd\blacksquare(\langle L\rangle+\langle R\rangle)}\rrbracket;\alpha^{+}_{(\mathbf{0}+\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket)+\llbracket{\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{rfix}(\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\delta^{-1}_{\Sigma};[\llbracket{\Gamma,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash t_{i}\rhd\langle\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\rangle}\rrbracket;\alpha^{+}_{(\mathbf{0}+\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket)+\llbracket{\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket},]_{i})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\mathsf{rfix}(\mathsf{lsem}_{\Gamma,\mathsf{L}}((\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}));\alpha^{+}_{\mathbf{0}+\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket}

Hence, we have that

(176)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma,\square:\langle\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\rangle\vdash\mathsf{case}\;\mathsf{ua}\;\square\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:\mathsf{br}\;\blacksquare\;x,\iota_{r}\;{y}:L\}\rhd\blacksquare([\mathsf{L}])}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket};\delta^{-1};[\llbracket{\Gamma,x:\langle\mathsf{L}\rangle\vdash\mathsf{br}\;\blacksquare\;x\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L}\rangle)}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\Gamma,y:\langle\mathsf{R}\rangle\vdash L\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L}\rangle)}\rrbracket]

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket};\delta^{-1};[\pi_{r};\alpha^{+}_{\mathbf{0}+\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket},\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\mathsf{rfix}(\mathsf{lsem}_{\Gamma,\mathsf{L}}((\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}));\alpha^{+}_{\mathbf{0}+\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket}]

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\delta^{-1};[\pi_{r},\mathsf{rfix}(\mathsf{lsem}_{\Gamma,\mathsf{L}}((\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}))];\alpha^{+}_{\mathbf{0}+\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket}

It hence suffices by completeness (Theorem
5.15
) to show that

(177)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{seq}(\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(r),\mathsf{case}\;\mathsf{ua}\;\square\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:\mathsf{br}\;\blacksquare\;x,\iota_{r}\;{y}:L\})\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L}\rangle)}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(r)\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\rangle)}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L,R}}\rrbracket};

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma,\square:\langle\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\rangle\vdash\mathsf{case}\;\mathsf{ua}\;\square\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:\mathsf{br}\;\blacksquare\;x,\iota_{r}\;{y}:L\}\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L}\rangle)}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{PW}_{\mathsf{L}}(r)\rhd\blacksquare(\langle\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\rangle)}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\delta^{-1};

\displaystyle[\pi_{r},\mathsf{rfix}(\mathsf{lsem}_{\Gamma,\mathsf{L}}((\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}))];\alpha^{+}_{\mathbf{0}+\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash r\;\mathsf{where}\;(\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\alpha^{+}_{\mathbf{0}+\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash[r\;\mathsf{where}\;(\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}]^{+}\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket

∎

B.3.
Substitution

Lemma B.2.

The following facts hold:

(a)

For all
f:A\to B\otimes C
,
g:(A\otimes B)\otimes C\to D
, we have

(178)

\mathsf{let}(f);\alpha;\mathsf{let}(g);(\pi_{l};\pi_{l})\otimes D=\mathsf{let}(\mathsf{let}(f);\alpha;g)

(b)

For all
f:A\to B+C
,
g:A\otimes B\to D
,
h:A\otimes C\to D
, we
have

(179)

\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};[\mathsf{let}(g);\pi_{l}\otimes D,\mathsf{let}(h);\pi_{l}\otimes D]=\mathsf{let}(\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};[g,h])

(c)

For all
f_{i}:R\otimes A_{i}\to B
, we have

(180)

\delta_{\Sigma}^{-1};[\mathsf{let}(f_{i});\pi_{l}\otimes B,]_{i}=\mathsf{let}(\delta_{\Sigma}^{-1};[f_{i}]_{i});\pi_{l}\otimes B

i.e.,

(181)

\delta_{\Sigma}^{-1};[\mathsf{rlet}(f_{i}),]_{i}=\mathsf{rlet}(\delta_{\Sigma}^{-1};[f_{i}]_{i})

(d)

For all
f:A\to B+C
,
g:A\otimes B\to B^{\prime}
,
h:A\otimes C\to C^{\prime}
, we have

(182)

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}(\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};g+h)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};[\mathsf{let}(g);\pi_{l}\otimes\iota_{l},\mathsf{let}(h);\pi_{l}\otimes\iota_{r}]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};(\mathsf{let}(g);\pi_{l}\otimes B^{\prime})+(\mathsf{let}(h);\pi_{l}\otimes C^{\prime});\delta

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};\mathsf{rlet}(g)+\mathsf{rlet}(h);\delta

In particular, we have

(183)

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}(\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};\pi_{r}+h)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};[A\otimes\iota_{l},\mathsf{let}(h);\pi_{l}\otimes\iota_{r}]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};(A\otimes B)+(\mathsf{let}(h);\pi_{l}\otimes C^{\prime});\delta

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};(A\otimes B)+\mathsf{rlet}(h);\delta

and

(184)

\displaystyle\mathsf{let}(\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};g+\pi_{r})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};[\mathsf{let}(g);\pi_{l}\otimes\iota_{l},A\otimes\iota_{r}]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};(\mathsf{let}(g);\pi_{l}\otimes B^{\prime})+(A\otimes C);\delta

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(f);\delta^{-1};\mathsf{rlet}(g)+(A\otimes C);\delta

\weakeninglem

*

Proof.

To show that variable weakenings compose
(a)
, we proceed by induction on the
derivation of
\Gamma\leq\Gamma^{\prime}
as follows:

•

wk-nil
: if
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}=\cdot
, then
\Delta=\cdot
, and so we trivially
have
\llbracket{\cdot\leq\cdot}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\cdot\leq\cdot}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\cdot\leq\cdot}\rrbracket=\mathsf{id}

•

wk-skip
: we have
\Gamma=\Xi,x:{A}^{\epsilon}
, and so by induction

(185)

\llbracket{\Xi,x:{A}^{\epsilon}\leq\Gamma^{\prime}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Delta}\rrbracket=\pi_{l};\llbracket{\Xi\leq\Gamma^{\prime}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Delta}\rrbracket=\pi_{l};\llbracket{\Xi\leq\Delta}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\Xi,x:{A}^{\epsilon}\leq\Delta}\rrbracket

as desired

•

wk-cons
: we have
\Gamma=\Xi,x:{A}^{\epsilon}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Xi^{\prime},x:{A}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}
. We proceed by case analysis on
\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Delta
:

–

wk-skip
: we have

(186)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Xi,x:{A}^{\epsilon}\leq\Xi^{\prime},x:{A}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Xi^{\prime}\leq\Delta}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Xi\leq\Xi^{\prime}}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Xi^{\prime},x:{A}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\leq\Delta}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Xi\leq\Xi^{\prime}}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\pi_{l};\llbracket{\Xi^{\prime}\leq\Delta}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\pi_{l};\llbracket{\Xi\leq\Xi^{\prime}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Xi^{\prime}\leq\Delta}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\pi_{l};\llbracket{\Xi\leq\Delta}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Xi,x:{A}^{\epsilon}\leq\Delta}\rrbracket

as desired

–

wk-cons
: we have
\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},x:{A}^{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}
, and so by
induction

(187)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Xi,x:{A}^{\epsilon}\leq\Xi^{\prime},x:{A}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Xi^{\prime},x:{A}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\leq\Delta^{\prime},x:{A}^{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Xi\leq\Xi^{\prime}}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Xi^{\prime}\leq\Delta^{\prime}}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=(\llbracket{\Xi\leq\Xi^{\prime}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Xi^{\prime}\leq\Delta^{\prime}}\rrbracket)\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Xi\leq\Delta^{\prime}}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Xi,x:{A}^{\epsilon}\leq\Delta}\rrbracket

as desired

We can analogously show
(b)
(i.e., that label weakenings compose) by induction on the
derivation of
\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}
as follows:

•

lwk-nil
: if
\mathsf{L}=\mathsf{K}=\cdot
, then
\mathsf{L}^{\prime}=\cdot
, so the result
follows trivially from the fact that
\llbracket{\cdot}\rrbracket=\mathbf{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}}
is the initial object

•

lwk-skip
: we have
\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\ell(A)
, and therefore

(188)

\llbracket{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\ell(A)}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}\rrbracket;\iota_{r}=\llbracket{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}\rrbracket;\iota_{r}=\llbracket{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

•

lwk-cons
: we have
\mathsf{L}=\mathsf{R},\ell(A)
and
\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\ell(A)
. We
proceed by case splitting on
\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}
:

–

lwk-skip
: we have

(189)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{R},\ell(A)}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{R},\ell(A)\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\ell(A)}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\iota_{r};\llbracket{\mathsf{R}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{A}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{R}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}\rrbracket;\iota_{r}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}\rrbracket;\iota_{r}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\ell(A)}\rrbracket

–

lwk-cons
: we have that
\mathsf{L}^{\prime}=\mathsf{R}^{\prime},\ell(A)
, and therefore

(190)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\mathsf{R}^{\prime},\ell(A)\leq\mathsf{R},\ell(A)}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{R},\ell(A)\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\ell(A)}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{R}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{A}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=(\llbracket{\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{R}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}\rrbracket)+\llbracket{A}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\mathsf{R}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{A}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\mathsf{R}^{\prime},\ell(A)\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\ell(A)}\rrbracket

We can now show weakening for expressions
\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}

(c)
by
induction on the typing derivation as follows:

•

var
: we need to show that

(191)

\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}x:{A}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\pi_{\Delta,x}=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}x:{A}}\rrbracket=\pi_{\Gamma,x}

we proceed by induction on
\Gamma\leq\Delta
:

–

wk-nil
: this case yields a contradiction, since if
\Delta=\cdot
it cannot
define
x
.

–

wk-cons
: given
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},y:{B}^{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}
,
\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},y:{B}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}
,

*

If
x=y
, then
B=A
, and

(192)

\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime},x:{A}^{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\leq\Delta^{\prime},x:{A}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}}\rrbracket;\pi_{(\Delta,x:{A}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}),x}=\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Delta^{\prime}}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\pi_{r}=\pi_{r}=\pi_{(\Gamma,x:{A}^{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}),x}

as desired.

*

Otherwise, we have by induction that

(193)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime},y:{B}^{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\leq\Delta^{\prime},y:{B}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}}\rrbracket;\pi_{(\Delta^{\prime},y:{B}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}),x}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Delta^{\prime}}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{B}\rrbracket;\pi_{l};\pi_{\Delta^{\prime},x}

\displaystyle=\pi_{l};\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Delta^{\prime}}\rrbracket;\pi_{\Delta^{\prime},x}=\pi_{l};\pi_{\Gamma^{\prime},x}=\pi_{\Gamma,x}

–

wk-skip
: we have
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},y:{B}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}
, and hence

(194)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime},y:{B}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\pi_{\Delta,x}=\pi_{l};\llbracket{\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\pi_{\Delta,x}=\pi_{l};\pi_{\Gamma^{\prime},x}=\pi_{\Gamma,x}

•

let
1
: we have

(195)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}\mathsf{let}\;x=a;\;b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\leq\Delta,x:A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}\mathsf{let}\;x=a;\;b:{B}}\rrbracket

•

unit
: follows immediately since weakenings are pure and
\mathbf{1}
is the terminal
object.

•

pair
: we have

(196)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}(a,b):{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;{\Delta}_{\Delta};\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{\Gamma};(\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket)\otimes(\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket)

\displaystyle={\Delta}_{\Gamma};\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}(a,b):{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket

•

let
2
: we have

(197)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=a;\;b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket);\alpha;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket);\alpha;\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{B}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket);\alpha;\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A,y:B\leq\Delta,x:A,y:B}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket);\alpha;\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=a;\;b:{B}}\rrbracket

•

case
: we have

(198)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:s,\iota_{r}\;{y}:t\}:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\delta^{-1};[\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}s:{B}}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\Delta,y:A\vdash_{\epsilon}t:{B}}\rrbracket]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\delta^{-1};

\displaystyle[\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}s:{C}}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{B}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}t:{C}}\rrbracket]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);

\displaystyle[\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\leq\Delta,x:A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}s:{C}}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\Gamma,y:B\leq\Delta,y:B}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}t:{C}}\rrbracket]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);[\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}s:{C}}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\Gamma,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}t:{C}}\rrbracket]

•

op
: we have

(199)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}f\;a:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{f\in\mathcal{I}_{\epsilon}(A,B)}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{f\in\mathcal{I}_{\epsilon}(A,B)}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}f\;a:{B}}\rrbracket

•

inl
,
inr
: analogous to the
op
case

Similarly, we can show weakening for regions
\Delta\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L}

(d)
by
induction on the typing derivation as follows:

•

br
: we have that

(200)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash\mathsf{br}\;\ell\;a\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\bot}a:{A}}\rrbracket;\iota_{\mathsf{L},\ell};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\bot}a:{A}}\rrbracket;\iota_{\mathsf{L},\ell};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

It hence suffices to show that

\iota_{\mathsf{L},\ell};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket=\iota_{\mathsf{K},\ell}
, which we can do by
induction on
\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}
:

–

lwk-nil
: this case yields a contradiction, since if
\mathsf{K}=\cdot
it cannot
define the label
\ell
.

–

lwk-skip
: we have
\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\kappa(B)
, and hence

(201)

\iota_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime},\ell};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\kappa(B)}\rrbracket=\iota_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime},\ell};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\kappa(B)}\rrbracket;\iota_{l}=\iota_{\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\ell};\iota_{l}=\iota_{\mathsf{K},\ell}

–

lwk-cons
: we have
\mathsf{L}=\mathsf{L}^{\prime},\kappa(B)
and
\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\kappa(B)

.

*

If
\kappa=\ell
, then
B=A
and

(202)

\iota_{(\mathsf{L}^{\prime},\ell(A)),\ell};\llbracket{\mathsf{L},\ell(A)\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\ell(A)}\rrbracket=\iota_{r};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{A}\rrbracket=\iota_{r}=\iota_{\mathsf{K},\ell}

*

Otherwise, we have by induction that

(203)

\displaystyle\iota_{(\mathsf{L}^{\prime},\kappa(B)),\ell};\llbracket{\mathsf{L},\kappa(B)\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\kappa(B)}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime},\ell};\iota_{l};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{B}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathsf{L}^{\prime},\ell};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}^{\prime}\leq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}\rrbracket;\iota_{l}

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathsf{K}^{\prime},\ell};\iota_{l}

\displaystyle=\iota_{\mathsf{K},\ell}

•

let
1
-r
: we have by induction that

(204)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash\mathsf{let}\;x=a;r\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\leq\Delta,x:A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{let}\;x=a;r\rhd\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

•

let
2
-r
: we have by induction that

(205)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=a;r\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket);\alpha;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A,y:B\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket);\alpha;\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{B}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A,y:B\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket);\alpha;\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A,y:B\leq\Delta,x:A,y:B}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A,y:B\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket);\alpha;\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A,y:B\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

•

case-r
: we have by induction that

(206)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:r,\iota_{r}\;{y}:s\}\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\delta^{-1};

\displaystyle[\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash s\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\Delta,y:B\vdash t\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket];\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\delta^{-1};[

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash s\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket,

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{B}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,y:B\vdash t\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\delta^{-1};[

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\leq\Delta,x:A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash s\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket,

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma,y:B\leq\Delta,y:B}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,y:B\vdash t\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket);\delta^{-1};[\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\vdash s\rhd\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\Gamma,y:B\vdash t\rhd\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket]

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:s,\iota_{r}\;{y}:t\}\rhd\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

•

cfg
: Let
L=\mathsf{lsem}_{\Delta,\mathsf{L}}((\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i})
and

\mathsf{R}=(\ell_{i}(A_{i}),)_{i}
. We have by induction that

(207)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;L;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\delta^{-1}_{\Sigma};[(\llbracket{\Delta,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash t_{i}\rhd\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket,)_{i}];\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\delta^{-1}_{\Sigma};[\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket;(\llbracket{\Delta,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash t_{i}\rhd\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\alpha_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket+\llbracket{\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket,)_{i}];\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=\delta^{-1}_{\Sigma};[(\llbracket{\Gamma,x_{i}:A_{i}\leq\Delta,x_{i}:A_{i}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash t_{i}\rhd\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\leq\mathsf{K},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket,)_{i}];\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=\delta^{-1}_{\Sigma};[(\llbracket{\Gamma,x_{i}:A_{i}\vdash t_{i}\rhd\mathsf{K},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket,)_{i}];\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{lsem}_{\Gamma,\mathsf{K}}((\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i})

It follows that

(208)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash r\;\mathsf{where}\;(\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}\rhd\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Delta\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket);\llbracket{\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{L}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket});\delta^{-1};[\pi_{r},\mathsf{rfix}(L)];\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{L}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket});\delta^{-1};[\pi_{r},\llbracket{\Gamma\leq\Delta}\rrbracket;\mathsf{rfix}(L)];\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\alpha^{+}_{\llbracket{L}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket});[\pi_{r};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket,\mathsf{rfix}(\mathsf{lsem}_{\Gamma,\mathsf{K}}((\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}))]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\alpha_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket);[\pi_{r},\mathsf{rfix}(\mathsf{lsem}_{\Gamma,\mathsf{K}}((\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}))]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\alpha_{\llbracket{\mathsf{L}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\mathsf{L}\leq\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket);[\pi_{r},\mathsf{rfix}(\mathsf{lsem}_{\Gamma,\mathsf{K}}((\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}))]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\mathsf{L},\mathsf{R}\leq\mathsf{K},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\alpha_{\llbracket{\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket});[\pi_{r},\mathsf{rfix}(\mathsf{lsem}_{\Gamma,\mathsf{K}}((\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}))]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{let}(\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash r\rhd\mathsf{K},\mathsf{R}}\rrbracket;\alpha_{\llbracket{\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket+\Sigma_{i}\llbracket{A_{i}}\rrbracket});[\pi_{r},\mathsf{rfix}(\mathsf{lsem}_{\Gamma,\mathsf{K}}((\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}))]

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash r\;\mathsf{where}\;(\ell_{i}(x_{i}):\{t_{i}\},)_{i}\rhd\mathsf{K}}\rrbracket

Weakening for substitutions
(e)
and label substitututions
(f)

then follow by a trivial induction.
∎

Lemma B.3 (Substitution Projection).

For
\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta
,
\mathsf{eff}(\Delta)=\bot
and
\Delta(x)=A
, we have

(209)

\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\pi_{\Delta,x}=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]x:{A}}\rrbracket

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
\Delta
:

•

If
\Delta=\cdot
, then
\Delta(x)=A
is a contradiction.

•

If
\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},x:A
, then
\gamma=\gamma^{\prime},x\mapsto[\gamma]x
, so we have

(210)

\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\pi_{\Delta,x}=\Delta_{\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta^{\prime}}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\bot}[\gamma]x:{A}}\rrbracket;\pi_{r}=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]x:{A}}\rrbracket

as desired.

•

If
\Delta=\Delta^{\prime},y:B
(with
y\neq x
), then
\gamma=\gamma^{\prime},y\mapsto[\gamma]y
, so by induction we have

(211)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\pi_{\Delta,x}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta^{\prime}}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\bot}[\gamma]y:{B}}\rrbracket;\pi_{l};\pi_{\Delta^{\prime},x}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\gamma^{\prime}:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta^{\prime}}\rrbracket;\pi_{\Delta^{\prime},x}=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma^{\prime}]x:{A}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]x:{A}}\rrbracket

∎

\soundnesssubst

*

Proof.

Fix
\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta
with
\mathsf{eff}(\Delta)=\bot
. We will begin by showing
the soundness of substitution for expressions
(a)
: we proceed by induction
on the derivation
\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}e:{E}
:

•

If
e=x
is a variable, then by Lemma
209
, we have

(212)

\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}x:{A}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\pi_{\Delta,x}=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]x:{A}}\rrbracket

as desired.

•

If
e=f\;a
is an operation, then by induction we have that

(213)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}f\;a:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{f\in\mathcal{I}_{B}(\epsilon,A)}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]a:{A}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{f\in\mathcal{I}_{B}(\epsilon,A)}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma](f\;a):{B}}\rrbracket

as desired. The cases for left injections, right injections, and
\mathsf{abort}
are analogous

•

If
e=(\mathsf{let}\;x=a;\;b)
is a unary
\mathsf{let}
-binding, then by induction we have that

(214)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{(\gamma,x\mapsto x):(\Gamma,x:A)\mapsto(\Delta,x:A)}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma,x\mapsto x]b:{B}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]b:{B}}\rrbracket

as we can assume that
x
is a fresh variable. Hence, it follows that

(215)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}\mathsf{let}\;x=a;\;b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\Delta_{\llbracket{\Delta}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]a:{A}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]a:{A}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma](\mathsf{let}\;x=a;\;b):{B}}\rrbracket

as desired.

•

If
e=(a,b)
is a pair, then by induction we have that

(216)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}(a,b):{A\times B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\Delta_{\llbracket{\Delta}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket\ltimes\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket};(\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A}}\rrbracket)\ltimes(\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{B}}\rrbracket)

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]a:{A}}\rrbracket\ltimes\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]b:{B}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma](a,b):{A\times B}}\rrbracket

•

If
e=(\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=a;\;b)
is a binary
\mathsf{let}
-binding, then by induction we have
that

(217)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes(\llbracket{A}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{B}\rrbracket);\alpha;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{C}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\alpha;\llbracket{(\gamma,x\mapsto x,y\mapsto y):(\Gamma,x:A,y:B)\mapsto(\Delta,x:A,y:B)}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{C}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma,x\mapsto x,y\mapsto y]b:{C}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]b:{C}}\rrbracket

as we can assume that
x,y
are fresh variables. Hence, it follows that

(218)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=a;\;b:{C}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\Delta_{\llbracket{\Delta}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket;\alpha;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{C}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]a:{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes(\llbracket{A}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{B}\rrbracket);\alpha;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{C}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]a:{A\otimes B}}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]b:{C}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma](\mathsf{let}\;(x,y)=a;\;b):{C}}\rrbracket

as desired.

•

If
e=\mathsf{case}\;a\;\{\iota_{l}\;{x}:b,\iota_{r}\;{y}:c\}
is a
\mathsf{case}
-expression, then by induction we have
that

(219)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{C}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{(\gamma,x\mapsto x):(\Gamma,x:A)\mapsto(\Delta,x:A)}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{C}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma,x\mapsto x]b:{C}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\Gamma,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]b:{C}}\rrbracket

and

(220)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{B}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}c:{C}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{(\gamma,y\mapsto y):(\Gamma,y:B)\mapsto(\Delta,y:B)}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}c:{C}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\Gamma,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma,x\mapsto x]c:{C}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\Gamma,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]c:{C}}\rrbracket

as we can assume that
x,y
are fresh variables. Hence, it follows that

(221)

\displaystyle\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket;\Delta_{\llbracket{\Delta}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Delta\vdash_{\epsilon}a:{A+B}}\rrbracket;\delta^{-1}_{\llbracket{\Delta}\rrbracket};[\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{C}}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\Delta,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}c:{C}}\rrbracket]

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket};\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{\Gamma\vdash_{\epsilon}[\gamma]a:{A+B}}\rrbracket;\delta^{-1}_{\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket};

\displaystyle[\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{A}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,x:A\vdash_{\epsilon}b:{C}}\rrbracket,\llbracket{\gamma:\Gamma\mapsto\Delta}\rrbracket\otimes\llbracket{B}\rrbracket;\llbracket{\Delta,y:B\vdash_{\epsilon}c:{C}}\rrbracket]
